Salvation begins by invitation only

Salvation by invitation shows us that you must be drawn:

John 6:44: No one can come to me unless the Father who sent me draws him; and I will raise him up on the last day.

John 6:41–51: 41 Therefore the Jews were grumbling about him, because he said, I am the bread that came down out of heaven. 42 They were saying, Is not this Jesus, the son of Joseph, whose father and mother we know? How does he now say, I have come down out of heaven? 43 Jesus answered and said to them, Do not grumble among yourselves. 44 No one can come to me unless the Father who sent me draws him; and I will raise him up on the last day. 45 It is written in the prophets, AND THEY SHALL ALL BE TAUGHT OF GOD. Everyone who has heard and learned from the Father, comes to me. 46 Not that anyone has seen the Father, except the one who is from God; he has seen the Father. 47 Truly, truly, I say to you, he who believes has eternal life. 48 I am the bread of life. 49 Your fathers ate the manna in the wilderness, and they died. 50 This is the bread which comes down out of heaven, so that one may eat of it and not die. 51 I am the living bread that came down out of heaven; if anyone eats of this bread, he will live forever; and the bread also which I will give for the life of the world is my flesh.

Salvation by invitation shows us that most are NOT drawn—and that is the clear intention of God:

Matthew 13:10–17: 10 And the disciples came and said to him, Why do you speak to them in parables? 11 Jesus answered them, To you it has been granted to know the mysteries of the kingdom of heaven, but to them it has not been granted. 12 For whoever has, to him more shall be given, and he will have an abundance; but whoever does not have, even what he has shall be taken away from him. 13 Therefore I speak to them in parables; because while seeing they do not see, and while hearing they do not hear, nor do they understand. 14 In their case the prophecy of Isaiah is being fulfilled, which says, YOU WILL KEEP ON HEARING, BUT WILL NOT UNDERSTAND; YOU WILL KEEP ON SEEING, BUT WILL NOT PERCEIVE; 15 FOR THE HEART OF THIS PEOPLE HAS BECOME DULL, WITH THEIR EARS THEY SCARCELY HEAR, AND THEY HAVE CLOSED THEIR EYES, OTHERWISE THEY WOULD SEE WITH

THEIR EYES, HEAR WITH THEIR EARS, AND UNDERSTAND WITH THEIR HEART AND RETURN, AND I WOULD HEAL THEM. 16 But blessed are your eyes, because they see; and your ears, because they hear. 17 For truly I say to you that many prophets and righteous men desired to see what you see, and did not see it, and to hear what you hear, and did not hear it.

Salvation by invitation shows us that the drawing is purposefully selective:

Matthew 11:25–26: 25 At that time Jesus said, I praise You, Father, Lord of heaven and earth, that You have hidden these things from the wise and intelligent and have revealed them to infants. 26 Yes, Father, for this way was well-pleasing in Your sight.

Salvation by just and merciful inclusion

Salvation by just and merciful inclusion shows us that ALL of humanity are included:

Romans 5:18: So then as through one transgression there resulted condemnation to all men, even so through one act of righteousness there resulted justification of life to all men.

Salvation by just and merciful inclusion shows us the vast plan of God for all of His human creation:

1 Timothy 2:1-6: 1 First of all, then, I urge that entreaties and prayers, petitions and thanksgivings, be made on behalf of all men, 2 for kings and all who are in authority, so that we may lead a tranquil and quiet life in all godliness and dignity. 3 This is good and acceptable in the sight of God our Savior, 4 who desires all men to be saved and to come to the knowledge of the truth. 5 For there is one God, and one mediator also between God and men, the man Christ Jesus, 6 who gave himself as a ransom for all, the testimony given at the proper time.

This part of salvation is God’s desire, but there is NOT a calling attached to it. Notice the order—be saved and THEN knowing the truth, whereas, for the average Christian (with whom we disagree), it is BELIEVE FIRST and then saved.

Salvation by just and merciful inclusion shows us that those invited are specially privileged:

1 Timothy 4:10: For it is for this we labor and strive, because we have fixed our hope on the living God, who is the Savior of all men, especially of believers.

There are two times of salvation

The thought behind the word “salvation” is to rescue and deliver.

Every human being born in Adam has need of salvation. The death sentence passed upon Adam was because of his disobedience in the Garden of Eden. This death sentence has been inherited by every human being.

Romans 5:12: Through one man sin entered into the world, and death through sin, and so death spread to all men, because all sinned.

Death is automatically inherited by every human being born to this earth. Alienation from God has resulted in not only death and physical problems and issues, but a perverse way of thinking and acting, which has led to all kinds of choices and actions including murder and war.

Ephesians 4:17-19: 17…walk no longer as the Gentiles also walk, in the futility of their mind, 18 being darkened in their understanding, excluded from the life of God, because of the ignorance that is in them, because of the hardness of their heart, 19 and they having become callous, have given themselves over to sensuality, for the practice of every kind of impurity with greediness.

But God has provided salvation and deliverance from this death sentence and process:

Acts 4:11-12: 11 He is the stone which was rejected by you, the builders, but which became the very corner stone. 12 And there is salvation in no one else; for there is no other name under heaven that has been given among men, by which we must be saved.

Just as all death came through Adam, so all hope of deliverance from death and depravity comes through Jesus:

1 Corinthians 15:22: (KJV) …as in Adam all die, even so in Christ shall all be made alive.

The Bible shows that while salvation is promised for ALL, it takes place in two different periods of time.

A resurrection of deliverance is promised for ALL:

1 Corinthians 15:22-23: 22 For as in Adam all die, so also in Christ all shall be made alive. 23 But each in his own order: Christ the first fruits, (faithful Christians) and after that those who are Christ’s at his coming (the rest of our human family, the "everybody else").

John 5:28-29: 28 Do not marvel at this: for an hour is coming, in which all who are in the tombs shall hear his voice 29 and shall come forth; those who did the good deeds (faithful Christians) to a resurrection of life, those who committed the evil deeds (the rest of our human family) to a resurrection of judgment.

First, salvation and deliverance is carried out for to those who believe and live a faithful life of obedience to Christ.

Hebrews 5:8-9: (KJV) 8 Though he were a son, yet learned he obedience by the things which he suffered; 9 and being made perfect, he became the author of eternal salvation unto all that obey him.

In Romans 1:16, the gospel of Christ is defined as the power of God unto salvation to everyone that believeth.

The end result of this salvation of faithful Christians and their heavenly reward is an even more widespread salvation, the salvation of souls (1 Peter 1:9), the deliverance of all the human family in a kingdom on earth.

Second is the phase of salvation called the “resurrection of judgment.” An earthly resurrection will provide the ability to understand the truth of Jesus and the Bible, a blessing of physical healing, and an opportunity to learn a lifestyle that leads to holiness in thought and character.

Isaiah 11:9: They will not hurt or destroy in all My holy mountain, for the earth will be full of the knowledge of the LORD as the waters cover the sea. See also Jeremiah 31:34.

The resurrection of judgment will lead to the full opportunity for all the effects of sin to be reversed.

Not only will all be able to SEE the truth and accept Christ, they will also be empowered to walk the "highway of holiness." This will be a symbolic journey of learning and a process of literal and spiritual healing, which will lead to the development of a character of holiness.

Isaiah 35:5-6,8: (KJV) 5 Then the eyes of the blind shall be opened, and the ears of the deaf shall be unstopped. 6 Then shall the lame leap as an hart, and the tongue of the dumb sing…8 and an highway shall be there, and a way, and it shall be called the way of holiness; the unclean shall not pass over it, but it shall be for those; the wayfaring men, though fools, shall not err therein.

Why is salvation important? Without this fullness of salvation there would be no hope or future for anyone!

Ephesians 1:9-10: (Weymouth) 9 And this is in harmony with God’s merciful purpose 10 for the government of the world when the times are ripe for it—the purpose which He has cherished in His own mind of restoring the whole creation to find its one Head in Christ: yes, things in heaven and things on earth, to find their one Head in him.

### Study Questions

https://christianquestions.com/doctrine/god-calling/

Who draws individuals to follow Christ?

1.

John 6:44 – How do we become true followers of Christ?

2. Matthew 11:25-26 – Who is called? Is it a universal call? Who receives salvation? When?

3. Matthew 11:28-30 – Who is invited in these verses? What are they promised?

4. Luke 9:23-24 – What does discipleship require? Is this a contradiction with point 3? Explain.

5. Deuteronomy 28:1-3 – What was Israel promised if they obeyed God? Explain whether these same promises

apply to us.

Motivation for seeking God - Despair:

1.

If we have a lack of hope or are in despair, what do we naturally seek?

2. Deuteronomy 28:1-3 – What was Israel promised if they obeyed God? Explain whether these same promises

apply to us.

3. Acts 10:2-5 – Who was Cornelius? Why was he a person God noticed? Why are we saying was he in despair?

4. Acts 10:27-29 – Why was Peter’s visit to Cornelius’ home controversial?

5. Acts 10:45-48 – How was Cornelius’ devotion to God rewarded? Why was this a surprise to Jewish Christians?

Motivation for seeking God – Awakening:

1.

Deuteronomy 28:4-6 – What was Israel promised?

2. Mark 10:20-22, Matthew 8:19-20 – What is the Christian’s reality?

3. Acts 9:1-6 – Saul loved God, but where did he go wrong? How was he awakened?

4. Acts 9:15-22 – How did Saul’s life change? Did the loss of his prestige and abundance stop his service to God?

Motivation for seeking God – Curiosity:

1.

Deuteronomy 28:7, John 15:18,20 – What was Israel promised? Are we promised this?

2. Matthew 5:13, Leviticus 2:13 – Why is salt important? How does it represent our character? How does God’s

providence protect the true Christian?

3. Luke 19:1-9 – Who was Zaccheus? Why was Jesus’ reaction to him “unexpected?” What was Zaccheus’

response? How was his life changed?

Motivation for seeking God – Fear:

1. Deuteronomy 28:9-10 – What was Israel promised if they walked in God’s ways?

2. 1 Peter 4:12,14 – Should we expect earthly peace? Romans 7:21-25 – what is our fight?

3. John 1:41-42 – How did Jesus entice Simon to follow him? Matthew 4:18-20 – What was Simon’s new vocation?

4. Luke 5:6-11 – What was Simon Peter’s fear? Did he find earthly peace? Explain.

Which of these possible motivations (or others) have sent you to God? How have you changed your focus on life— from your normal earthly wants, needs and fears to the development of your spiritual desire to serve God? Where can you improve in this?

## Episode 1364 — Is the Gospel a Success or a Failure?
Published: 2024-12-16
Episode page: https://christianquestions.com/doctrine/gospel-success/
Rewind PDF: https://resources.christianquestions.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/20083747/CQ_Rewind_12-16-2024_Is_the_Gospel_a_Success_or_a_Failure.pdf

Matthew 9:35: Jesus was going through all the cities and villages, teaching in their synagogues and proclaiming the gospel of the kingdom, and healing every kind of disease and every kind of sickness.

Jesus was here 2,000 years ago. His message was clear as he had come to pay the price for Adam’s sin. Jesus preached, healed, called some to follow him and gave people hope. He called himself “the way, the truth and the life” and announced the “kingdom of God was at hand.” Fast-forward 2,000 years, and we have to ask, where are we? It’s plainly obvious that the world has not come around to follow Jesus. And to be blunt, it is also obvious that much of the history of Christianity does not reflect well on the principles that Jesus lived by and taught. So, what conclusions should we draw from all of this? Has the gospel message failed? Has the redemption Jesus promised the world just gone by the wayside, or is the message of the gospel doing precisely what God had always intended it to do?

Let’s start off by answering the question in our title: The gospel of God through the teachings of Jesus and the whole Bible has, is and forever will be precisely the overwhelming success God planned it to be.

What exactly does gospel mean? Sometimes, it simply refers to the books of Matthew, Mark, Luke or John. More deeply, it means the “good news” that Jesus preached—a beautiful message about God’s plan for ALL people, which has many aspects. Some of these are Jesus’ sacrifice, the earth’s restoration, Satan’s destruction and God’s future kingdom that will bless all of creation.

The context of the first mention of the word gospel in the New Testament is in the Gospel of Mark. This use introduces the word and defines its breadth.

First, the definition:

Mark 1:1: The beginning of the gospel <2098> of Jesus Christ, the son of God.

Gospel: Strong’s Exhaustive Concordance #2098 euaggelion; from the same as #2097; a good message, i.e. the gospel

Strong’s Exhaustive Concordance #2097 euaggelizo; (from #2095—good and #32 messenger—bring tidings); to announce good news ("evangelize") especially the gospel

A part of the word in Hebrew comes from the same word translated as angel or messenger, so it means “to bring tidings.” “Tidings” is an old-fashioned word for recent news.

Here is one example of how this word is used:

Luke 2:10: But the angel <32> said to them, Do not be afraid; for behold, I bring <2097> you good news <2097> of great joy which will be for all the people.

The King James Version says—I bring you good tidings of great joy. So, the gospel is good news that needs to be announced! Along with the birth of Jesus came the heavenly proclamation that good news—the gospel —would be joyous AND apply to all of humanity.

Next, Mark will show us the breadth and basis of this gospel:

Mark 1:1-4: 1 The beginning of the gospel <2098> of Jesus Christ, the son of God. 2 As it is written in Isaiah the prophet: (Mark then quotes from Malachi 3:1 and Isaiah 40:3) BEHOLD, I SEND MY MESSENGER AHEAD OF YOU, WHO WILL PREPARE YOUR WAY; 3 THE VOICE OF ONE CRYING IN THE WILDERNESS, MAKE READY THE WAY OF THE LORD, MAKE HIS PATHS STRAIGHT. 4 John the Baptist appeared in the wilderness preaching a baptism of repentance for the forgiveness of sins.

From these scriptures, we see the gospel of Jesus Christ had its foundation in the Old Testament. The Old Testament and New Testament are linked together, forming the basis of the gospel.

John the Baptist is considered the last of the Old Testament prophets. His job was to prepare the way for the gospel of Jesus Christ.

Now, we will give an overview of how Jesus himself described and lived the gospel. First, he described it:

Mark 1:14-17: 14 Now after John had been taken into custody, Jesus came into Galilee, preaching the gospel <2098> of God, 15 and saying, The time is fulfilled, and the kingdom of God is at hand; repent and believe in the gospel <2098>. 16 As he was going along by the Sea of Galilee, he saw Simon and Andrew, the brother of Simon, casting a net in the sea; for they were fishermen. 17 And Jesus said to them, Follow me, and I will make you become fishers of men.

Jesus’ description of the gospel was marked by the kingdom of God being at hand, by repenting and by believing.

Then Jesus showed us what this gospel would ultimately bring by his living and giving its blessings:

Matthew 9:35-38: 35 Jesus was going through all the cities and villages, teaching in their synagogues and proclaiming the gospel <2098> of the kingdom, and healing every kind of disease and every kind of sickness. 36 Seeing the people, he felt compassion for them, because they were distressed and dispirited like sheep without a shepherd. 37 Then he said to his disciples, The harvest is plentiful, but the workers are few. 38 Therefore beseech the Lord of the harvest to send out workers into his harvest.

In the context of Jesus proclaiming the gospel message, we learn a little more. The gospel was marked by:

the use of miracles,

the need for shepherding the people, and

the need for a harvest. There are not many workers, yet ripe fields to pick.

Jesus also introduced what the gospel would cost those who would become his disciples:

Mark 8:34-35: 34 And he summoned the crowd with his disciples, and said to them, If anyone wishes to come after me, he must deny himself, and take up his cross and follow me. 35 For whoever wishes to save his life will lose it, but whoever loses his life for my sake and the gospel’s <2098> will save it.

The gospel would also require major self-sacrifice from those who would carry its message with Jesus. He wanted his followers to understand that spreading the gospel would be a mission that would include many aspects.

At this point, the foundational gospel teachings certainly look like a lot more than the typical Christian view of repent, believe, be saved and be blessed!

So far, we have a view of the gospel from Jesus’ preaching and life. Did it change once Jesus had ascended into heaven?

After Jesus paid the ransom price, his human existence ended and he once again became a spirit being—only this time he had been elevated to God’s right hand of power. What would happen on earth through his disciples would dramatically show us what the mission of the gospel would look like in his absence. While on earth, Jesus was the example of preaching and living the gospel, but he literally handed off his gospel mission to his disciples at his death.

His last words of guidance to them before his ascension to heaven are recorded in

Matthew 28:16-20, Luke 24:44-49 and Acts 1:6-11. Here we will sum up all three sources and fit them together into one narrative that will define the success of the gospel.

We will begin by looking at the ascension:

Acts 1:6-7: 6 So when they had come together, they were asking him, saying, Lord, is it at this time you are restoring the kingdom to Israel? 7 He said to them, It is not for you to know times or epochs which the Father has fixed by His own authority…

Jesus instructed them not to expect things to happen according to their expectations. They were supposed to wait, listen and follow without jumping to conclusions. This would have been a difficult concept for the disciples to grasp, since Jesus had been appearing to them after he had been raised from the dead. They were anxious to understand the timing of God’s plan.

Acts 1:8: But you will receive power when the holy spirit has come upon you; and you shall be my witnesses both in Jerusalem, and in all Judea and Samaria, and even to the remotest part of the earth.

Jesus unfolded their role in the work of the gospel: Once they received God’s power through the holy spirit, they were to preach what they learned to all—even to the remotest part of the earth. He gave them the responsibility to be his witnesses and do this work since he would no longer be visible as a man.

Matthew 28:18-20: And Jesus…spoke to them, saying, All authority has been given to me in heaven and on earth. 19 Go therefore and make disciples of all the nations, baptizing them in the name of the Father and the son and the holy spirit, 20 teaching them to observe all that I commanded you…

He told them not only to preach, but to find those who would follow him. This is the harvest work he previously spoke of—followers of Jesus would be “harvested.”

Notice the phrase used is make disciples of all the nations, NOT make all nations my disciples. This is a subtle but important point.

Let’s continue with our combined account from Acts, Luke and Matthew:

Acts 1:9: 9 And after he had said these things… Luke 24:50: …He lifted up his hands and blessed them. Matthew 28:20: …lo, I am with you always, even to the end of the age. Luke 24:51: While he was blessing them, he parted from them and was carried up into heaven.

His final gospel preparation message to his disciples was an assurance of his presence with them. This must have been a dramatic scene! At this point, Jesus had been raised from the dead and had been on earth with them for 40 days. He was blessing them as he was being carried up into heaven. This incredible experience would have left an indelible impression on their minds; they were literally witnesses to his ascension. But his last words were an assurance that his presence would remain with them as they continued the gospel work.

So, what does all of this mean? Was the mission of the gospel to convert the world, or was it to preach to the world?

It was not to convert at this point. In the Parable of the Sower, Jesus taught that all would NOT have a fair chance at conversion.

Some pointed parable observations:

A farmer sows seeds.

Some fall along the path, some onto stony places, some into thorny places and some into good ground.

Jesus and his followers are pictured in the sower.

The Parable of the Sower is recorded in Matthew, Mark and Luke.

Let’s begin with the account in Luke:

Luke 8:11: Now the parable is this: the seed is the word of God.

Jesus explained the parable starting in Matthew 13.

Looking ahead at one aspect of this parable’s explanation helps us see a selective calling:

Matthew 13:19: When anyone hears the word of the kingdom and does not understand <4920> it, the evil one comes and snatches away what has been sown in his heart. This is the one on whom seed was sown beside the road.

Understand: Strong’s Exhaustive Concordance #4920 suniemi; from #4862 and hiemi (to send); to put together, i.e. (mentally) to comprehend; by implication, to act piously

The King James Version translates as “consider,” “understand,” “be wise.”

This Greek word for understand means “to comprehend.” We often hear things we don’t understand or comprehend. “Understanding” means we have something under us to stand on—a support or foundation. Stability comes from an appreciation and a comprehension of the facts.

The gospel has a message that appeals to the heart. But emotion is a fickle thing. The head must also be involved. Jesus says this first group had a heart reaction, but they did not understand the message. Satan (represented as “hungry birds” in the Matthew and Mark accounts) snatches away what has been sown. Throughout the time the gospel message has gone out, many have been briefly attracted to it, but they go no further. They have no desire to spend time reasoning about it or learning deep things. They are successfully thwarted by Satan.

A simple lack of interest or understanding, along with Satan’s actions, keep many away from the gospel!

Why isn’t a personal rejection of the message given as a reason for being kept in the dark? Why is Satan blamed if, in fact, the individuals pictured here by those with the seed sown beside the road are given free choice?

Is it correct to say that some people don’t deserve the gospel because their hearts are not good? Because they are just too sinful? No!

The parable says that for some, Satan snatches understanding away. God does not dismiss those who had no control over the circumstances. Some people are born into disfunctional families with only negative role models, some have mental health challenges, some have no real contact with the gospel due to geography or the political climate, etc.

The point with the seed beside the road is that the individual did not, with full opportunity, personally reject the message. Some will just not be in the cirucmstances to understand, and Satan is blamed for the message not getting through.

This is further shown in Jesus’ explanation to his disciples about why he spoke in parables:

Mark 4:10-12: 10 As soon as he was alone, his followers, along with the twelve, began asking him about the parables. 11 And he was saying to them, To you has been given the mystery of the kingdom of God, but those who are outside get everything in parables, 12 so that (he next quotes from Isaiah 6:9-10) WHILE SEEING, THEY MAY SEE AND NOT PERCEIVE, AND WHILE HEARING, THEY MAY HEAR AND NOT UNDERSTAND, OTHERWISE THEY MIGHT RETURN AND BE FORGIVEN.

Notice this additional bridge between the Old and New Testaments.

If you want your message to be understood by all, a successful marketing campaign doesn’t present an obscure puzzle that requires a major dedication of time and effort to figure out! The key is understanding Jesus’ mission. It was NOT to change the hearts of every person he met at that time. He knew everyone would not become a follower; not everyone would try to understand. At this point, he was looking only for those whose ears, hearts and minds were open to his message. Most would walk away.

How is this fair?

By quoting from Isaiah 6:9-10, Jesus confirmed the prophecy came true. The gospel message was NOT meant for everyone—at this time! This was not the only time for the opportunity for the world to accept the gospel. It was not yet the time for Jesus’ disciples to “harvest” the entire world.

Jesus continued talking to his disciples:

Matthew 13:16-17: 16 But blessed are your eyes, because they see; and your ears, because they hear. 17 For truly I say to you that many prophets and righteous men desired to see what you see, and did not see it, and to hear what you hear, and did not hear it.

Those who did receive and comprehend the message would change their lives and follow his teachings.

We know there has to be more to the timing of the gospel message so as not to contradict the angel’s message of good tidings of great joy to ALL people. It’s not “good news” if billions of humans are lost!

The deeper we go into how the gospel message works, the more complex it seems to become. Obviously, we need to keep digging.

For many, Jesus dying for everyone, and yet only a few being given the privilege of understanding doesn’t make sense. How do we explain it?

The good news here is that we don’t have to explain it. Instead, we can just go to the Scriptures and let them do all of the talking. As we dive into this scriptural reasoning, we will touch on both subtle explanations as well as blatantly obvious ones. Let’s begin with the subtle side of things as we connect the Old and New Testaments.

The writers of the New Testament were all unified in their understanding of the gospel blessing different people in different ways at different times.

Here the Apostle Paul subtly builds on prophecy:

2 Corinthians 6:1-2: 1 …we also urge you not to receive the grace of God in vain— 2 for He says, (he next quotes Isaiah 49:8) AT THE ACCEPTABLE TIME I LISTENED TO YOU, AND ON THE DAY OF SALVATION I HELPED YOU. (He then explains when these are:) Behold, now is THE ACCEPTABLE TIME, behold, now is THE DAY OF SALVATION.

Who is Paul speaking to, and when is “now”?

The common view of many Christians is that right now in this lifetime is the only day of salvation. They believe if someone does not accept the gospel in this present life (even though Satan “snatched it away,” and Jesus clearly taught that some people were not meant to understand it at this time), there would be no further opportunity for salvation.

Paul was writing to dedicated Christians who sacrificed their lives to serve God through Christ. Paul was saying to them (and us!), This is your time for salvation, right here and now! You don’t have the future to wait for. You must be faithful now because this is your calling.

Is “now” in this lifetime the ONLY time of salvation for everyone? Let’s read the Isaiah text Paul quoted from:

Isaiah 49:8: Thus says the LORD, In a favorable time I have answered you, and in a day of salvation I have helped you; and I will keep you and give you for a covenant of the people, to restore the land, to make them inherit the desolate heritages.

At the time of Isaiah, Israel’s day of salvation this text refers to happened when they were restored to their land. They had experienced a time of punishment for disobedience, but now they were released—given “salvation”—and brought back to their land. This was “a” day of salvation for that generation.

But was that the only day of salvation? No! Paul moved the Old Testament fulfillment forward into the New Testament, applying it to “the” day of salvation for faithful followers of Jesus. Additional scriptures we will review shortly will move it forward again for the rest of the world.

Salvation (the application of the ransom sacrifice of Jesus, meaning being acquitted from the inherited sin of Adam) is not applied at the same time for all people. The Apostle Paul refers to Israel being given a day of salvation to restore to them their inherited land in Isaiah 49:8—that was one day of salvation. But there are other applicable times. The day of salvation for Jesus’ true followers is during their present lives. They receive the benefit of the ransom first. The rest of the world will receive the benefit of the ransom later after their resurrection in the future kingdom.

In the same epistle, Paul shows us what Jesus taught about some NOT having opportunity now:

2 Corinthians 4:3-4: 3 And even if our gospel <2098> is veiled, it is veiled to those who are perishing, 4 in whose case the god of this world has blinded the minds of the unbelieving so that they might not see the light of the gospel <2098> of the glory of Christ, who is the image of God.

The god of this world who has blinded the minds of the unbelieving refers to Satan. Paul confirms what Jesus taught in the the Parable of the Sower, with Satan snatching away the seed before it can take root.

These aren’t people who are asking to be blinded to the light of the gospel. They aren’t begging to follow Satan. It is Satan making the decisions, and they are victims of his rule. He is overwhelming them, veiling the gospel. What happens to these people? God’s wisdom developed a path to salvation for the entire world, as several scriptures plainly tell us.

Romans 8:19-21: 19 For the anxious longing of the creation waits eagerly for the revealing of the sons of God. 20 For the creation was subjected to futility, not willingly, but because of Him who subjected it, in hope 21 that the creation itself also will be set free from its slavery to corruption into the freedom of the glory of the children of God.

Two groups of people are described:

1. the creation—the world in general, waiting for the other group

2. the sons of God

It further describes the creation will be set free from slavery because of these sons or children of God. This sounds hopeful! This is the big picture of the plan of God—restoring mankind back to a relationship with Him that Adam lost in the Garden of Eden.

All generations after Adam were born in sin beyond their control. Why would God allow this? Because the permission of evil will result in a great and lasting learning experience. In the future, the creation will be set free from its slavery to corruption, just like the sons of God (Jesus’ footstep followers) have been.

We have two different groups of people, BOTH finding a great freedom and glory.

God’s power gives everyone an equal opportunity to be included in salvation for ALL:

1 Timothy 2:3-6: (KJV) 3 For this is good and acceptable in the sight of God our Saviour; 4 who will have all men to be saved, and to come unto the knowledge <1922> of the truth. 5 For there is one God, and one mediator between God and men, the man Christ Jesus; 6 who gave himself a ransom for all, to be testified in due time.

Knowledge: Strong’s Exhaustive Concordance #1922 epignosis; recognition, i.e. (by implication) full discernment

It is God’s will that all men will be saved by Jesus and come to the knowledge of full understanding. Of course, where there is understanding, there is also accountability.

Notice the order in verse 4: It’s not that all men come to the knowledge of the truth and THEN they are saved; rather it is, all men are saved and THEN come to a full discernment of the truth!

There will obviously be a lot of teaching and instruction taking place after the resurrection. After each person has been educated in godly truth, he or she will then have an opportunity to decide if they will continue along a righteous path in God’s kingdom or not.

Satan's influence during this time will not be permitted to interfere with the truth (Revelation 20:2-3). It's all about education, not indoctrination!

In one circumstance, Satan controls—the unbelieving world is blinded. In the next circumstance, Satan is not relevant, and God controls. They are “saved” first through Jesus’ ransom and THEN are brought to a knowledge of the truth.

How can we be sure ALL are included?

1 Timothy 4:10: For it is for this we labor and strive, because we have fixed our hope on the living God, who is the Savior of all men, especially of believers.

Again we see the two groups:

How is God everyone’s Savior? It is through His son, Jesus:

1 John 2:1-2: 1 My little children, I am writing these things to you so that you may not sin. And if anyone sins, we have an advocate with the Father, Jesus Christ the righteous; 2 and he himself is the propitiation (satisfaction) for our sins; and not for ours only, but also for those of the whole world.

The two groups again:

We saw the Apostle Paul writing on this theme, then the Apostle John writing on the same theme. We will see the theme continues from the Apostle Peter. These two groups are talked about again and again in Scripture.

So, if the followers of Jesus will not be able to convert the whole world at this time, what is our mission?

We are called and chosen to preach the gospel and to live the gospel every day:

1 Peter 2:9: But you are A CHOSEN RACE, A royal PRIESTHOOD, A HOLY NATION, A PEOPLE FOR God's OWN POSSESSION, so that you may proclaim the excellencies of Him who has called you out of darkness into His marvelous light.

Peter quotes from Exodus 19:5-6, applying the descriptions of the Jewish people to the Gentile Christians:

a chosen race a royal priesthood a holy nation a people for God’s own possession.

A priesthood, for example, exists and operates to serve and help others. This is what we are called to do. Even though we do not need to convert everyone, we are still to preach and live the gospel message!

We are to live a life that reflects the grace that has been given to us as disciples:

1 Peter 2:10: For you once were NOT A PEOPLE, but now you are THE PEOPLE OF GOD; you had NOT RECEIVED MERCY, but now you have RECEIVED MERCY.

You once were not a people, but now you are the people of God—since God called you and gave you His holy spirit (His power and influence), you are in a privileged, wonderful position.

Our living as examples of Jesus will later bless those who know of us now in this life:

1 Peter 2:12: Keep your behavior excellent among the Gentiles, so that in the thing in which they slander you as evildoers, they may because of your good deeds, as they observe them, glorify God in the day of visitation.

What and when is the day of visitation?

The day of visitation is synonymous with the Day of Judgment, the future time when God’s principles reign on earth through Jesus and his faithful followers, the true church.

Resurrected mankind will remember those who did their best to follow Jesus in this lifetime and will finally “get” it. They will be in a position to glorify God by understanding the examples of those faithful whom they slandered. (Hint there is no hellfire: no one can glorify God if they are in eternal flames of agony and torture.)

Remember, it’s education, not indoctrination, during the day of visitation. Today, we live in a world where Satan indoctrinates and avoiding sin is not a choice. Education in the kingdom will be a growth process. The gospel message will enable all who embrace it to love, understand, appreciate and follow God's will and way.

What is the essence of our preaching? Is it built on the Scriptures, or is it built on tradition?

The gospel has focused us on a level of good news that is rarely taught in any religion or belief system throughout the world.

The remarkable truths that are revealed by simply reading and piecing together biblical scriptures are immense! How do we tie it all together?

Frankly, tying this all together is a delightful challenge. We will focus on one more set of New Testament scriptures and then verify all of this by going back to the Old Testament and quoting just a few of the many prophecies that point us to the future of God’s kingdom for all.

Jesus gave every individual who seeks after him the answer in the simplicity of the Lord’s prayer:

Matthew 6:9-10: 9 Pray, then, in this way: Our Father who is in heaven, hallowed be Your name. 10 Your kingdom come. Your will be done, on earth as it is in heaven.

Your will be done, on earth as it is in heaven—is God’s will being done everywhere on earth right here, right now? No. Jesus is telling us to look forward and pray for this to happen on earth through the plan of God.

The good news of the gospel in action:

2 Corinthians 5:18-19: OUR ROLE AS TRUE CHRISTIANS 18 Now all these things are from God, who reconciled us to Himself through Christ and gave us the ministry of reconciliation,

We have been given the ministry of reconciliation. If we are given a ministry to reconcile something, there must be somebody who needs it.

THE WORLD’S BENEFIT: 19 namely, that God was in Christ reconciling the world to Himself, not counting their trespasses against them,

OUR ROLE: ...and He has committed to us the word of reconciliation.

Again, there are two groups:

The gospel in action—good tidings of great joy, which shall be to all people.

Presenting the clarity of the gospel and its application to every human life: THE WORLD’S BENEFIT:

Romans 5:15, 17-18: 15 But the free gift is not like the transgression. For if by the transgression of the one (Adam) the many died (everyone dies), much more did the grace of God and the gift by the grace of the one man, Jesus Christ, abound to the many (everyone who dies).

How “many” died? ALL. We all die. Jesus’ sacrifice is for ALL who have been touched by Adam’s sin.

OUR ROLE: 17 For if by the transgression of the one, death reigned through the one, much more those who receive the abundance of grace and of the gift of righteousness will reign in life through the one, Jesus Christ.

This is the promise and the privilege given to the called-out ones—to reign with Christ as a royal priesthood.

The Apostles Paul and Peter are in agreement, but they teach it from different perspectives so we, as diverse individuals, can understand our role.

THE WORLD’S BENEFIT: Here is the universal application to all of the human race! 18 So then as through one transgression there resulted condemnation to all men, even so through one act of righteousness there resulted justification of life to all men.

Romans 8:19-22: OUR ROLE: 19 For the anxious longing of the creation waits eagerly for the revealing of the sons of God. 20 For the creation was subjected to futility, not willingly, but because of Him who subjected it,

THE WORLD’S BENEFIT in hope 21 that the creation itself also will be set free from its slavery to corruption into the freedom of the glory of the children of God. 22 For we know that the whole creation groans and suffers the pains of childbirth together until now.

The Scriptures are clear on the two groups—the gospel works through the disciples of Jesus for the benefit of the world!

What does being set free from slavery into the freedom of glory look like to the whole world? It is a freedom beyond imagination.

- It is freedom from sin, death and all of the faultiness of all our human reasonings.
- It is a freedom from sickness, pain and sorrow.
- It is the freedom to be capable and responsible, to step up to the highest version of ourselves.

Psalm 22 prophetically details Jesus's suffering on the cross, starting with, My God, my God, why have you forsaken me? Reading through the Psalm details the horrors of the cross experience.

However, we don’t want to miss the victory described at the end of the Psalm:

Psalms 22:27-29: 27 All the ends of the earth will remember and turn to the LORD, and all the families of the nations will worship before You. 28 For the kingdom is the LORD’s and He rules over the nations. 29 All the prosperous of the earth will eat and worship, all those who go down to the dust will bow before Him, even he who cannot keep his soul alive.

To which we respond, Thy kingdom come!

All the prosperous of the earth—this is from an earthly perspective. This has been the message from the very beginning, but it was lost in all the sin and error-filled traditions of men. The world has lost the goodness—the core of the gospel message—which is phenomenally successful once we understand how it unfolds in phases.

Here is a different perspective on what this looks like for the world of mankind:

Isaiah 45:22-24: 22 Turn to Me and be saved, all the ends of the earth; for I am God, and there is no other. 23 I have sworn by Myself, the word has gone forth from My mouth in righteousness and will not turn back, that to Me every knee will bow, every tongue will swear allegiance.

This message goes out to all the ends of the earth. This takes place after resurrection on earth.

24 They will say of Me, Only in the LORD are righteousness and strength. Men will come to Him, and all who were angry at Him will be put to shame.

Education, not indoctrination. The “full disclosure” will bring shame for past actions but also beautiful opportunity to move forward. Once there is true understanding—a foundation on which to stand—humanity can be positive, proactive and productive because God’s will is reigning in the earth!

Mankind will understand what sin is. They will know what they have done wrong, who wronged them and how they have wronged others, and yes, there will be consequences and accountability, but with the goal of complete rehabilitation and restoration back to God.

Daniel 7:13-14: 13 …And behold, with the clouds of heaven one like a son of man was coming, and he came up to the Ancient of Days and was presented before Him. 14 And to him was given dominion, glory and a kingdom, that all the peoples, nations and men of every language might serve him. His dominion is an everlasting dominion which will not pass away; and his kingdom is one which will not be destroyed.

This is on planet Earth, and an everlasting dominion is the essence of the gospel as spoken in prophecy.

Habakkuk 2:14: For the earth will be filled with the knowledge of the glory of the LORD, as the waters cover the sea.

Again, this prophecy takes place on earth.

For the true footstep followers of Jesus, their time to accept salvation is now.

For the rest of the world, their time to accept salvation will be in the future, after the resurrection, after learning about the righteousness of God’s plan for them— full discernment—after rehabilitation, and finally under conditions that give them a fair chance at eternal life.

Is the gospel a success or a failure? The Bible tells us it is a resounding success! The Old Testament foretells what would happen in the New Testament and in the future.

Look at how Jesus put it all in order, how he handed it off to his followers and how their responsibility in the kingdom will be to reconcile the world back to God, just as Adam and Eve were created in the beginning. This has been God’s plan all along.

And that, our friends, is the gospel of Jesus Christ.

So, is the gospel a success or failure?

For Rick, Jonathan, Julie and Christian Questions… …Think about it!

All scriptures cited are from the NASB1995 translation unless otherwise noted.
