Here are a few more scriptures that highlight the role the gospel plays in our lives:

The challenges that come when the pure word of God meets the corrupting power of the ego:

Galatians 1:6-12: 6 I am amazed that you are so quickly deserting him who called you by the grace of Christ, for a different gospel <2098>; 7 which is really not another; only there are some who are disturbing you and want to distort the Gospel <2098> of Christ. 8 But even if we, or an angel from heaven, should preach <2097> to you a gospel <2097> contrary to what we have preached <2097> to you, he is to be accursed! 9 As we have said before, so I say again now, if any man is preaching <2097> to you a gospel <2097> contrary to what you received, he is to be accursed! 10 For am I now seeking the favor of men, or of God? Or am I striving to please men? If I were still trying to please men, I would not be a bond-servant of Christ. 11 For I would have you know, brethren, that the gospel <2098> which was preached <2097> by me is not according to man. 12 For I neither received it from man, nor was I taught it, but I received it through a revelation of Jesus Christ.

The power of the gospel message is positively worth suffering for:

2 Timothy 1:8-11: 8 Therefore do not be ashamed of the testimony of our Lord or of me his prisoner, but join with me in suffering for the gospel <2098> according to the power of God, 9 who has saved us and called us with a holy calling, not according to our works, but according to His own purpose and grace which was granted us in Christ Jesus from all eternity, 10 but now has been revealed by the appearing of our savior Christ Jesus, who abolished death and brought life and immortality to light through the gospel <2098> 11 for which I was appointed a preacher and an apostle and a teacher.

The gospel is even part of our Christian armor:

Ephesians 6:14-20: 14 Stand firm therefore, HAVING GIRDED YOUR LOINS WITH TRUTH, and HAVING PUT ON THE BREASTPLATE OF RIGHTEOUSNESS, 15 and having shod YOUR FEET WITH THE PREPARATION OF THE GOSPEL <2098> OF PEACE; 16 in addition to all, taking up the shield of faith with which you will be able to extinguish all the flaming arrows of the evil one. 17 And take THE HELMET OF SALVATION, and the sword of the spirit, which is the word of God. 18 With all prayer and petition pray at all times in the spirit, and with this in view, be on the alert with all perseverance and petition for all the saints, 19 and pray on my behalf, that utterance may be given to me in the opening of my mouth, to make known with boldness the mystery of the gospel <2098>, 20 for which I am an ambassador in chains; that in proclaiming it I may speak boldly, as I ought to speak.

### Study Questions

https://christianquestions.com/doctrine/gospel-success/

1. What does “gospel” mean? What was the foundation for the gospel Jesus taught? How do we know? Who is considered the last Old Testament prophet to prepare the way for the gospel of Jesus? (Luke 2:10,

Mark 1:1-4)

2. How did Jesus describe what was required of those living the gospel? What would the gospel eventually achieve? What would it require from Jesus’ followers? (Mark 1:14-17, Matthew 9:35-38, Mark 8:34-35)

3. What was the mission Jesus outlined for his disciples? Be thorough in your description. What was Jesus’ final promise to his followers? (Acts 1:6-11, Matthew 28:16-20, Luke 24:44-49)

4. In the Parable of the Sower, who will have a fair chance at appreciating the gospel?

a. What is the seed?

b. Who is pictured as the sower?

c. What happens to the seed sown beside the road? Who is to blame for this? (Matthew 13:3-9,16-23, Mark 4:14-20, Luke 8:5-8)

5. Whose hearts are supposed to be changed by the gospel of Jesus at this time? Is now the only time of salvation for everyone? Explain. What happens with the unbelieving? (2 Corinthians 4:3-4, 6:1-2,

Isaiah 49:8)

6. Who are the two groups described in Romans 8:19-21? What is the order of events for ALL men to come to a full discernment of the gospel? Why won’t Satan try to derail this? (1 Timothy 2:3-6, Revelation 20:23)

7. Who are the two groups in each of the following scriptures? (1 Timothy 2:3-6, 4:18, 1 John 2:10)

8. What is the role of the faithful Christian now? How does 1 Peter 2:9-12 describe our mission at this time?

9. Define the Benefit to the World and Our Role as True Christians in the following texts. Identify the two

groups in each:

a. 2 Corinthians 5:18-19

b. Romans 5:15, 17-18

c. Romans 8:19-22

10. Describe how God’s kingdom, as ruled by Jesus and his followers, will change the earth. How does this vision of a restored mankind here on earth affect your understanding of whether the gospel is a success or failure? (Psalms 22:27-29, Isaiah 45:22-24, Daniel 7:13-14, Habakkuk 2:14)

## Episode 1365 — Why Did the Angel Say “Fear Not”?
Published: 2024-12-23
Episode page: https://christianquestions.com/inspiration/1365-fear-not/
Rewind PDF: https://resources.christianquestions.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/12/27132035/CQ-Rewind_12_23_24_Why_Did_the_Angel_Say_Fear_Not.pdf

Luke 2:10–11: (KJV) 10 And the angel said unto them, Fear not: for, behold, I bring you good tidings of great joy, which shall be to all people. 11 For unto you is born this day in the city of David a Saviour, which is Christ the Lord.

When Jesus came to earth 2,000 years ago, he came to pay the price for Adam’s sin by willingly offering himself as a just and equal ransom price to redeem Adam and, therefore, the entire human race from sin and death. To prepare for his coming as a man, God sent the angel Gabriel to deliver preparatory messages to John the Baptist’s father, Zacharias, and to Jesus’ mother, Mary. Joseph would be guided by spiritually-driven dreams. When Jesus was born, a whole host of angels made the announcement to some lowly shepherds who were in the fields with their flocks. When this angelic announcement came, it was prefaced with some beautiful words of assurance: Fear not! This reassuring “fear not” sentiment would echo repeatedly throughout Jesus’ entire ministry!

There is a much bigger lesson behind the immediate need for the angel saying, Fear not!

Luke 2:8–10: (KJV) 8 And there were in the same country shepherds abiding in the field, keeping watch over their flock by night. 9 And, lo, the angel of the Lord came upon them, and the glory of the Lord shone round about them: and they were sore afraid. 10 And the angel said unto them, Fear <5399> not: for, behold, I bring you good tidings of great joy, which shall be to all people.

We all know what fear is. It means to be frightened, terrified, to be seized with alarm. The Greek word here is phobeō. Does that sound familiar? In English, a “phobia” is an anxiety disorder involving excessive and persistent fear of a situation or object.

NOTE: All uses of the word fear in the scriptures quoted in this episode are from this Greek word:

Fear: Strong’s Exhaustive Concordance #5399 phobeō; to frighten, i.e. (passively) to be alarmed; by analogy, to be in awe of, i.e. revere

Thayer’s Greek-English Lexicon: 1) to put to flight by terrifying (to scare away) 1b1) to be struck with fear, to be seized with alarm

The King James Version translates this word as; fear 62 times, be afraid 23 times, be afraid of 5 times, reverence 1 time, miscellaneous 2 times

Why did the angel begin his proclamation with “Fear not”?

The angel came to the shepherds unannounced. They were afraid. But the bigger message was “fear not” what’s about to be proclaimed: This is happening right now; it is from God, and it is for you. Have faith, courage and peace, because you are protected and loved in all these things!

Now let’s finish what the angels were proclaiming:

Luke 2:11–14: (KJV) 11 For unto you is born this day in the city of David a Saviour, which is Christ the Lord. 12 And this shall be a sign unto you; Ye shall find the babe wrapped in swaddling clothes, lying in a manger. 13 And suddenly there was with the angel a multitude of the heavenly host praising God, and saying, 14 Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will toward men.

These are lowly, humble shepherds—really “nobodies” in society. Imagine the fear, thrill and hope to have this privilege of hearing the message in the still of the night, under the vast expanse of the star-lit heavens.

It would have been amazing, and the initial fear would have been overwhelming. But the reassurance of the angels gave them the courage to move forward and look for the child. The “fear not” message helped them move forward, playing a necessary role in preparing those who would surround Jesus at the beginning of his earthly ministry.

We will briefly look at the “fear not” experiences of Zacharias and then Mary, the mother of Jesus.

The dramatic announcement of John the Baptist’s birth to his father, Zacharias:

Luke 1:11–13: (KJV) 11 And there appeared unto him an angel of the Lord standing on the right side of the altar of incense. 12 And when Zacharias saw him, he was troubled, and fear fell upon him. 13 But the angel said unto him, Fear not, Zacharias: for thy prayer is heard; and thy wife Elisabeth shall bear thee a son, and thou shalt call his name John.

This mighty angel came, again completely unannounced and unexpectedly, and the initial fear is immediately met with Fear not, you don't need to be afraid. This was followed by a message of comfort and guidance as to what's coming. No need to fear; I am delivering a message from God. It comes to you as a blessing and an assurance. Incidentally, what's going to happen with you and Elisabeth, with the birth of your son John, is going to begin to change the entire world!

In our next example, for the young woman Mary, the events that were to unfold around her would be world-changing.

After appearing to Zacharias, the angel Gabriel would later appear to her with startling news:

Luke 1:26–29: (KJV) 26 And in the sixth month the angel Gabriel was sent from God… 27 To a virgin espoused to a man whose name was Joseph, of the house of David; and the virgin's name was Mary. 28 And the angel came in unto her, and said, Hail, thou that art highly favoured, the Lord is with thee: blessed art thou among women. 29 And when she saw him, she was troubled at his saying, and cast in her mind what manner of salutation this should be.

Unlike with the shepherds, this angelic visit didn’t immediately start with a “fear not.” Instead, Mary was first told in a beautiful introduction that she was highly favored and blessed. Yet Mary’s reaction is one of confusion and fear, as she didn’t understand what the angel meant.

The angel continued with needed assurance:

Luke 1:30–33: (KJV) 30 And the angel said unto her, Fear not, Mary: for thou hast found favour with God. 31 And, behold, thou shalt conceive in thy womb, and bring forth a son, and shalt call his name JESUS. 32 He shall be great, and shall be called the Son of the Highest: and the Lord God shall give unto him the throne of his father David: 33 And he shall reign over the house of Jacob for ever; and of his kingdom there shall be no end.

How does one respond t0 this? Mary must have thought: You’re telling me I’m going to have a son who will be called the son of the Highest, who will be a king forever? The Messiah everyone has been waiting for is going to be born to ME? Yes, a “fear not” is definitely needed here! Maybe even a “Don’t panic!”

All three of these angelic encounters—with the shepherds, with Zacharias and now especially with Mary—were beyond the normal human experience.

There is no need to fear, Mary. This spiritual message has come to you to strengthen and assure you!

The angel continued to explain how this miracle would take place and then ended his message with more miraculous news:

Luke 1:36–37: (KJV) 36 And, behold, thy cousin Elisabeth, she hath also conceived a son in her old age: and this is the sixth month with her, who was called barren. 37 For with God nothing shall be impossible.

The angel gives Mary the details of what will happpen to her, and then comforts her with the news that she will not go through this experience alone. Her cousin, Elisabeth, beyond normal childbearing age, is also pregnant.

Fear not, but Gabriel ends his message with, ...for with God, nothing shall be impossible. This is the complete message of what "fear not" means in the scriptures describing the events of Jesus’ birth. We need to be calm in the moment to see the glory of God unfold.

We have credibility behind "fear not" because of who God is. When a messenger from God delivers God’s word, we can be assured “fear not” is a full assurance, a full guarantee.

With all God was doing at that time, one truth shines out with amazing clarity; God takes care of those individuals used to further His will!

These experiences were mighty examples of God’s powerful purposes being revealed. Did this “fear not” encouragement continue during Jesus’ ministry?

It should be no surprise that the “fear not” assurance continued. As we look into Jesus’ ministry, we’ll see a pattern of his assurances to his followers leading to their spiritual growth. Putting all of these things in order will teach us some very deep “fear not” lessons!

Jesus said Fear not to his followers many times. We will share seven “fear not” points that reveal a progression of tremendous lessons for each stage of the Christian walk.

If we do feel fear, we’re in good company. Simon (who became the Apostle Peter) was drawn by Jesus three separate times over a period of months before he fully and finally followed him.

Let’s look at Simon’s third drawing to Jesus. Jesus was in Simon's boat preaching to a large crowd. They had been fishing all night but caught nothing. So, the preaching was done, the fishing from the previous night was done, and they think: Okay, we're done. Well, not exactly.

Here’s what happened next:

Luke 5:4–7: 4 When he had finished speaking, he said to Simon, Put out into the deep water and let down your nets for a catch. 5 Simon answered and said, Master, we worked hard all night and caught nothing, but I will do as you say and let down the nets.

(Source: Life Application Study Bible Notes) Fisherman on the Sea of Galilee used nets, often bell-shaped ones with lead weights around the edges. A net would be thrown flat onto the water, and the lead weights would cause it to sink around the fish. Then the fishermen would pull on a cord, drawing the net around the fish.

Peter was an experienced fisherman listening to a carpenter telling him how to do his job! But he trusted Jesus enough to try.

6 When they had done this, they enclosed a great quantity of fish, and their nets began to break; 7 so they signaled to their partners in the other boat for them to come and help them. And they came and filled both of the boats, so that they began to sink.

Can you imagine their reaction? Stop everything! This large catch is an emergency! If the nets break, we will lose all these valuable fish! There are so many fish that the boats are sinking! What do we do? How is this even possible?

Here's a lesson for us: being overwhelmed and unable to comprehend something can contribute to fear. Even though they had seen Jesus perform miracles, this was too much to take in! It was happening to them personally. Jesus’ power and influence through God’s spirit were unmistakable, used to produce the miracle of the fish just for them.

Simon Peter paid attention!

Luke 5:8–11: 8 But when Simon Peter saw that, he fell down at Jesus’ feet, saying, Go away from me Lord, for I am a sinful man! 9 For amazement had seized him and all his companions because of the catch of fish which they had taken; 10 and so also were James and John, sons of Zebedee, who were partners with Simon. And Jesus said to Simon, Do not fear (KJV: Fear not), from now on you will be catching men. 11 When they had brought their boats to land, they left everything and followed him.

I can relate to Peter. He told Jesus he was a sinful man not worthy of being called. For me, I came from a sinful, worldly background. Plus, my family has a history of mental illness. When our heavenly Father called me to follow His son, like Peter, I also was scared and didn't feel worthy or capable. God showed me that if I was willing, He could certainly work all things out in me. That doesn't mean I don't get anxious trying my best to serve Him.

We want faith to replace our fear. This is what Simon Peter had to learn. To teach him that lesson, Jesus said essentially two important words: Fear not! You don't have to be afraid. I'm here. I know what I'm doing.

There's a tremendous assurance that says you don't have to worry; God has drawn you. Jesus is saying to us through Scripture, I know who you are and what you're capable of, and I know that if you can just put your faith in me as your Lord and Master, you can progress and move forward. (And that was Jonathan's experience in his calling.)

Once committed, we now strive to put aside our fears as we acknowledge and trust the leading of God through our Lord Jesus.

Psalm 32:8: I will instruct you and teach you in the way which you should go; I will counsel you with My eye upon you.

In other words, God’s providence will be with you. This is why Jesus saying fear not is so reassuring—it allows us to access the heavenly Father.

This verse is God’s assurance of His personal direction and guidance. But just because we follow His direction doesn’t mean our lives will change overnight. On the contrary, we often experience the very same doubts, fears and inconsistencies as we did previously. This can accelerate our natural fears. Fear not! You don’t need to be afraid.

Jesus knew fear was the natural response of broken humanity, and he explained our new reality in the context of the Sermon on the Mount.

Many people were listening, but he's talking to his followers and teaching them what a Christlike life should look like:

Luke 12:25,27–32: 25 And which of you by worrying can add a single hour to his life’s span? 27 Consider the lilies, how they grow: they neither toil nor spin; but I tell you, not even Solomon in all his glory clothed himself like one of these. 28 But if God so clothes the grass in the field, which is alive today and tomorrow is thrown into the furnace, how much more will He clothe you? You men of little faith! 29 And do not seek what you will eat and what you will drink, and do not keep worrying. 30 For all these things the nations of the world eagerly seek; but your Father knows that you need these things. 31 But seek His kingdom, and these things will be added to you. 32 Do not be afraid (KJV: Fear not), little flock, for your Father has chosen gladly to give you the kingdom.

Jesus is saying, Look at all those stressors in your lives that you’re not going to be able to change just by working harder, getting fearful, being anxious, trying harder or running faster. Fear not! Your life is now about following me. There is something different guiding your life. This is the power of following after Jesus.

What about those who have a harder time managing anxiety than others? God recognizes it might be harder for them to embrace the peace that comes with “Fear not.”

(We recommend listening to Episode #1274: “If I Have God’s Peace, Why Am I So Stressed?” and Episode #1124: “Does My Anxiety or Depression Invalidate My Christianity?”)

Many of us have overwhelming anxiety and depression, which is difficult. We might discount ourselves thinking, I can't do this or that. But we can! God doesn’t expect us to be like anyone else—we are all different. He will work with us through our difficulties, helping us.

I know many Christians with anxiety and depression and those who've gone through suicidal tendencies. My greatest respect for my fellow Christians goes to those who deal with those issues because I see the fight. I know that by God's grace, even though it feels like progress is not being made, the Lord looks at the heart and the effort and says, “Don't be afraid. I've got you in this.” We need to embrace His message.

God's peace needs to become a companion as we work through these things, and that's why Jesus repeats the phrase Fear not! so many times to his followers.

The power of Jesus calling us to follow him and assuring us of our ability to overcome through God’s peace is such encouragement.

We can see how reassuring Jesus is as we begin our journey of discipleship. What do his assurances bring us as we mature in Christ?

One of the many beautiful facets of walking as a true disciple of Christ is that this journey is surrounded by encouragement and strength. No matter what stage or difficulty of the journey we may be experiencing, we will always be guided by solid, faith-strengthening truth and encouragement.

Viewing our physical circumstances through God’s eyes teaches us to face our human circumstances with HIS courage.

Joshua needed this perspective when he stepped into the impossible task of leading Israel:

Joshua 1:9: Have I not commanded you? Be strong and courageous! Do not tremble or be dismayed, for the LORD your God is with you wherever you go.

Joshua was given no small task, but along with that assignment he was given a tremendous promise. Moses had died and the nation of Israel needed a leader. Joshua answered this call, but it was overwhelming. He needed to stand up in God’s strength. Do not tremble or be dismayed, for the LORD your God is with you wherever you go.

A life of discipleship is a life of service. We are called to give, extend ourselves and engage in any part of the work of the gospel as God directs us. Fear not! You don’t need to be afraid. Jesus knew how daunting this could be and prepared his followers for the work.

Jesus had to prepare his apostles because he was sending them out to preach for the first time without him.

Jesus outlined three specific things they (and we!) should not fear:

“FEAR NOT” PERSECUTION:

Matthew 10:24–31: 24 A disciple is not above his teacher, nor a slave above his master. 25 It is enough for the disciple that he become like his teacher, and the slave like his master. If they have called the head of the house Beelzebul, how much more will they malign the members of his household! 26 Therefore do not fear (KJV: fear them not) them, for there is nothing concealed that will not be revealed, or hidden that will not be known.

Jesus informs his disciples they shouldn’t be surprised if they are persecuted, since he was persecuted. It is not something to fear, as God will judge each individual in the Day of Judgment with an appropriate consequence. The disciples should just move forward with what they are given to do.

“FEAR NOT” WHEN PREACHING: 27 What I tell you in the darkness, speak in the light; and what you hear whispered in your ear, proclaim upon the housetops. 28 Do not fear (KJV: Fear not) those who kill the body but are unable to kill the soul; but rather fear Him who is able to destroy both soul and body in Gehenna.

There would come a time when what Jesus told them in private was to be proclaimed, without fear, in public. Although the people listening might rebel against what the disciples are saying, they have no power over the disciples’ lives. This is a powerful “fear not” statement to rely on in our preaching.

“FEAR NOT” BEING FORGOTTEN: We all sometimes feel alone or minimized and unimportant. 29 Are not two sparrows sold for a cent? And yet not one of them will fall to the ground apart from your Father. 30 But the very hairs of your head are all numbered. 31 So do not fear (KJV: fear ye not); you are more valuable than many sparrows.

Don’t forget that God is watching over the details of our lives. Remember the verse we previously read in Psalm 32:8, I will counsel you with My eye upon you. We are not alone, no matter what.

Do not fear; you are more valuable than many sparrows. God sees the small details of our lives, and these details are in His hands. Jesus tells us, Fear not! You are more valuable than all of these other things God has under His control. Do not be overwhelmed.

Sometimes, we are in circumstances where we just “need” to know what the next chapter is. As the experience unfolds, we realize all we need to know is our next small step because all of it is in His hands. We do not need to be afraid.

Hard testings: These trials are difficult on purpose because learning to be like Jesus does not happen in an environment of ease and comfort.

With these challenges come promises of deliverance through the experience—not that it will be removed entirely:

1 Corinthians 10:13: No temptation has overtaken you but such as is common to man; and God is faithful, who will not allow you to be tempted beyond what you are able, but with the temptation will provide the way of escape also, so that you will be able to endure it.

Whatever our circumstances, there is a way through it. The promise is for strength and fortitude to endure all the way through any experience. Some of the difficulties and traumas go on for years and years. This promise applies to those as well. God does not usually just “snap away” our difficulties. He works us through them because that is the hard testing required for us to grow into being a faithful disciple of Jesus. This is a beautiful promise that as we fight the good fight, we do not have to be afraid.

An example of hard testing is when the apostles were in a boat at night without Jesus. The winds and waves battered that boat. They rowed hard but got nowhere. The boat was filling with water, and they fought hard to keep it stable.

Jesus appears, but not in a natural way:

Matthew 14:25–29: 25 And in the fourth watch of the night he came to them, walking on the sea. 26 When the disciples saw him walking on the sea, they were terrified, and said, It is a ghost! And they cried out in fear.

Of course they were terrified! The fourth watch of the night is between 3am and 6am. It’s dark, they’re exhausted and now they’re seeing someone or something they don’t recognize coming towards them on the water!

27 But immediately Jesus spoke to them, saying, Take courage, it is I; do not be afraid (KJV: do not be afraid) 28 Peter said to him, Lord, if it is you, command me to come to you on the water. 29 And he said, Come! And Peter got out of the boat, and walked on the water…

These are experienced men who are on the sea, and they feel like they are going to sink. There is an overwhelming fear. Jesus comes along and says, Take courage, it is I; do not be afraid, and his mere presence miraculously changes their environment.

Sometimes it feels like we are rowing hard, but the boat of our lives fills up and we go nowhere. We get frustrated and anxious. But Jesus is saying, Fear not! Let me get in the boat with you.

We can see how significant the phrase “Fear not!” the angel spoke at the birth of Jesus was and how it stretches all the way through Jesus’ ministry. Fear not in the hard testings.

These are the blessings that leave us with a speechless wonderment at the glory of God, and His granting us a part in His glorious plan.

1 Corinthians 2:9: (KJV) But as it is written, Eye hath not seen, nor ear heard, neither have entered into the heart of man, the things which God hath prepared for them that love him.

We can’t even begin to comprehend the grandeur of God’s plans and purposes for us!

This is the other side of the protection of “fear not.” There are blessings in our lives beyond belief.

The privilege Christian Questions has to talk about the Bible with whoever will listen is a blessing beyond our comprehension. “Fear not” is such an important aspect of our lives in that it allows us to be fearless and move forward.

In our next example, Jesus brought Peter, James and John up to the Mount of Transfiguration. They had NO idea what they were in for.

What began as terrifying became a blessing beyond their comprehension:

Matthew 17:5–7: 5 While he was still speaking, a bright cloud overshadowed them, and behold, a voice out of the cloud said, This is My beloved son, with whom I am well-pleased; listen to him! 6 When the disciples heard this, they fell face down to the ground and were terrified. 7 And Jesus came to them and touched them and said, Get up, and do not be afraid (KJV: be not afraid).

This overwhelming vision and powerful voice from heaven were too much for those men, and they fell down terrified. Jesus came to them and touched them—he let them know he was there with them in the midst of the vision. While Jesus doesn’t physically touch us, he “touches” us through many of our experiences and helps us find the faith to face our fear.

We have all these wonderful indications of our Lord’s plan for us, and our reaction after we catch our breath should be to stand up and honor the power of God that guides us, delivers us and moves us forward through His plan.

The comprehensiveness of the “fear not” assurances Jesus brings us at every stage of our lives is truly astounding!

It is obvious that Jesus encourages us to be without fear in all of our experiences. Where does this fearlessness bring us?

The destination of these “fear not” encouragements is Christian maturity. It is learning the hard lessons of life and growing through them as our faith gradually takes a firmer hold on our hearts and minds. As we will see, the whole purpose of Jesus telling us to “fear not” is so we can stand firm in faith and reflect the grace and glory of our Lord Jesus!

We need to develop “spiritual fearlessness,” even if we naturally have a lot of anxiety. God blesses our efforts to work through whatever challenges we have.

Over time, the spiritual maturity that comes as a result of this sanctifying process actually changes who we are—we are transformed.

Philippians 4:6–7: 6 Be anxious for nothing, but in everything by prayer and supplication with thanksgiving let your requests be made known to God. 7 And the peace of God, which surpasses all comprehension, will guard your hearts and your minds in Christ Jesus.

There is power in the peace of God, which surpasses all comprehension. This peace doesn’t negate feelings of fear. They still come, but it will help us dissolve our fears into spiritually-driven actions. It is a learning process which becomes a maturing process.

(Source: Devotional from Pastor Craig Groeschel, “Missing Peace”) That doesn’t mean we’ll always feel happy or that we’ll never experience anxiety or fear. It does mean that we know how to find peace when we struggle, because peace is a byproduct of God’s presence…The good news is that peace isn’t found in the absence of problems. It’s found in the presence of Jesus. The Greek word for peace is eirene, which also means wholeness. Jesus didn’t just come so we could feel better some days. He came to restore us— and our relationship to God—to wholeness.

Or as our CQ volunteer Tamra says, “Jesus is the missing piece to our complete peace!”

After Jesus died and was raised, he appeared to the women who went to the tomb to anoint his body and pay their respects. They found an empty tomb. An angel gave them instructions to leave and tell the disciples Jesus was risen.

Jesus appeared as they were on their way:

Matthew 28:10: Then Jesus said to them, Do not be afraid (KJV: Be not afraid): go and take word to my brethren to leave for Galilee, and there they will see me.

The women had left the tomb, bewildered, thinking about what the angel had told them to do. Then Jesus appeared, and his first words were Fear not!

He was essentially saying, Here is what I want you to do with this bewilderment and numbness at this moment. I will show you this is all within God’s glorious plans. This helped them find the faith to face their fear.

We might have questions, but Jesus’ response is, Fear not! You are working within the confines of accomplishing God’s plan. You do not have to be overwhelmed because you are in His hands.

Fear not! is a powerful message. Jesus said it throughout his earthly ministry and beyond. We saw it when he drew Simon Peter at the beginning of his ministry. The message doesn’t change after his resurrection, as it continues to be relevant to us here and now. We don’t need to be afraid. We don’t have to fear. Fear not!

Following Jesus on this path of self-sacrifice is the destiny of every disciple.

We are to walk this way until there are no more steps left for us to take:

Romans 8:31–32: 31 What then shall we say to these things? If God is for us, who is against us? 32 He who did not spare His own son, but delivered him over for us all, how will He not also with him freely give us all things?

If God is for us, who is against us? According to this promise, the path to Christlikeness supplies all that we need to keep walking!

Jesus even gave the Apostle Paul a direct “fear not” proclamation, even though Paul became the 12th Apostle long after Jesus took his position at the right hand of God’s power. This was at a time when Paul was in the exceptionally wicked city of Corinth and needed encouragement.

(Source: Bible Commentary by David Guzik) Corinth was a major city of the Roman Empire, at an important crossroads of trade and travel. It was also a city notorious for its hedonism and immorality. In classical Greek, to act like a Corinthian meant to practice fornication…One ancient writer described Corinth as a town where “none but the tough could survive.”

Acts 18:8–11: 8 Crispus, the leader of the synagogue, believed in the Lord with all his household, and many of the Corinthians when they heard were believing and being baptized. 9 And the Lord said to Paul in the night by a vision, Do not be afraid (KJV: Do not be afraid) any longer, but go on speaking and do not be silent; 10 for I am with you, and no man will attack you in order to harm you, for I have many people in this city. 11 And he settled there a year and six months, teaching the word of God…

Do not be afraid (fear not) implies Paul WAS afraid—we can assume he was afraid of the Jews who opposed him here and because the city itself was dangerous. His message to repent and clean up their lives to follow Jesus would not be popular in this spiritually- polluted atmosphere.

No men will attack you to harm you—it didn’t mean they would go away entirely, just that he would be protected through the experience. He ended up teaching there for 18 months. He was fully committed to the church at Corinth.

Anyone can be a peaceful person. However, we are talking about having peace with God.

Ultimately, to be at peace with God individually, we must go through Jesus:

Romans 5:1-2: (NASB) 1 Therefore, having been justified by faith, we have peace with God through our Lord Jesus Christ, 2 through whom also we have obtained our introduction by faith into this grace in which we stand; and we exult in hope of the glory of God.

To have peace with God, we need to grow into the peace of God.

In conclusion:

Colossians 3:12–17: 12 So, as those who have been chosen of God, holy and beloved, put on a heart of compassion, kindness, humility, gentleness and patience; 13 bearing with one another, and forgiving each other, whoever has a complaint against anyone; just as the Lord forgave you, so also should you.

These are all needed in order to have peace with God. They are part of the “fear not” equation of our Christian lives. They make sure we see things through the eyes of God through Christ.

14 Beyond all these things put on love, which is the perfect bond of unity.

It is scary to love unconditionally, but Fear not! We do not need to be afraid. Jesus showed us how.

15 Let the peace of Christ rule in your hearts, to which indeed you were called in one body; and be thankful.

It is scary to let go of our individuality and become part of his body of believers, but Fear not! We do not need to be afraid. This is our calling.

16 Let the word of Christ richly dwell within you, with all wisdom teaching and admonishing one another with psalms and hymns and spiritual songs, singing with thankfulness in your hearts to God.

Sometimes it is scary to let go of the way we want to see things, the way we are used to thinking, the way we would like it to all come out. Instead, let the word of Christ dwell within us. It is scary, but we do not have to be afraid.

17 Whatever you do in word or deed, do all in the name of the Lord Jesus, giving thanks through him to God the Father.

Moving aside our own ego can be scary. Fear not! I don’t have to be afraid. I can do things in the name of Jesus instead of my own name.

Luke 12:32: (KJV) Fear not, little flock; for it is your Father’s good pleasure to give you the kingdom.

Do we truly understand the magnitude of this? And yet, the message is not to be afraid.

Fear not! What is about to be proclaimed, or what is happening right now in your life, is from God and is for you. Have faith, have courage and have peace, as you are protected and loved in all these things.

- At his birth, the message proclaiming Jesus was, Fear not! - Throughout the life of his disciples, the message was, Fear not! - Right now at this time, the message is, Fear not!

Why don’t we need to fear? Because it is God’s plan and purpose working in you. Do not be afraid!

So, why did the angel say “Fear not”?

For Rick, Jonathan, Julie and Christian Questions… …Think about it!

All scriptures cited are from the NASB1995 translation unless otherwise noted.
